# LLM4GIS: Methods, Applications, and Prospects

[![Awesome](https://img.shields.io/badge/Awesome-LLM4GIS-F65021?style=for-the-badge&logo=awesome-lists&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/LuojiaGeoCode/Awesome-LLM4GIS)

> A curated list of research on **large language model-driven GIS analysis (LLM4GIS)**, accompanying our survey *LLM4GIS: Methods, Applications, and Prospects*. It covers **673 research papers published between 2022 and July 2026**, organized by technical approaches, datasets, applications, and evaluation.
>
> We welcome everyone to open an issue for any related work we haven't discussed, and we'll try to address it in the next release!

## 📊 Bibliometric Analysis

We built the corpus through a multi-source, iterative, manual identification and screening process (literature retrieved up to **July 31, 2026**). A study was included when (1) an LLM, multimodal LLM, or LLM-driven agent serves as a core reasoning or generation component; (2) geospatial data, GIS tasks, Earth observation, or urban/transportation/environmental processes are the research object; and (3) the work makes a substantive methodological, empirical, or evaluative contribution. Preprints and their formal versions are merged so that **each study is counted once**, yielding **673 core research papers** (558 general studies and 115 evaluation studies).

- **Rapid growth.** 3 papers in 2022 → 21 in 2023 → 114 in 2024 → 317 in 2025 → 218 in the first seven months of 2026.
- **Venues.** 118 studies remained arXiv-only at the cutoff, and another 134 were first released on arXiv before formal publication. ACM SIGSPATIAL (39) is the largest formal venue, followed by ISPRS IJGI (25), IJGIS (25), ACL (23), and IJDE (21).
- **Geography.** By first-author affiliation, China (317, 47.1%) and the United States (158) lead, followed by Germany, the United Kingdom, Japan, and Australia.
- **Methods.** Prompt engineering remains dominant, while RAG, LLM-based agents, architecture design, tokenization, and reinforcement learning are growing quickly.

## 🧩 Taxonomy and Selected Papers

The taxonomy follows the structure of the survey and spans four dimensions: **technical approaches, datasets, applications, and evaluation**.

- 1. Introduction
- 2. LLM4GIS
  - 2.1 Evolution of GIS Analysis Methods
  - 2.2 Concept of LLM4GIS
  - 2.3 Research Development and Trends
- 3. Technical Approaches in LLM4GIS
  - 3.1 Geospatial-Aware Architecture Design
    - 3.1.1 Tokenization
    - 3.1.2 Model Architecture
  - 3.2 Geospatial-Specialized Model Training
    - 3.2.1 Pretraining
    - 3.2.2 Supervised Fine-Tuning
    - 3.2.3 Reinforcement Learning
  - 3.3 Geospatial-Oriented Utilization Strategies
    - 3.3.1 Prompt Engineering
    - 3.3.2 Retrieval-Augmented Generation
    - 3.3.3 LLM-Based Agents
- 4. Datasets
  - 4.1 Dataset Construction (Open-Source Curation, Programmatic Generation, LLM Synthesis, Expert Annotation, Crowdsourcing)
- 5. Applications
  - 5.1 Geospatial Knowledge Q&A
  - 5.2 Geospatial Knowledge Extraction
  - 5.3 Spatiotemporal Reasoning
  - 5.4 Geospatial Code Generation
  - 5.5 Geospatial Analysis Modeling
- 6. Evaluation
  - 6.1 Evaluation for Geospatial Knowledge Q&A
  - 6.2 Evaluation for Geospatial Knowledge Extraction
  - 6.3 Evaluation for Spatiotemporal Reasoning
  - 6.4 Evaluation for Geospatial Code Generation
  - 6.5 Evaluation for Geospatial Analysis Modeling
- 7. Challenges and Prospects
- 8. Conclusions

Representative papers discussed in the survey are listed below (a paper may appear under several categories). For every included paper, see the [Full Paper List](#%EF%B8%8F-full-paper-list-all-673-papers).

## 🔭 Challenges and Prospects

LLM4GIS is moving from early application-oriented exploration toward systematic methodological innovation. The survey identifies five key directions:

1. **Unified integration of multimodal spatiotemporal data.** Text, remote sensing imagery, vector, raster, and trajectory data differ in structure, coordinate reference systems, and spatiotemporal resolution, calling for unified representation and access mechanisms.
2. **High-quality data and standardization.** Annotated GIS data for pretraining, fine-tuning, and evaluation remain scarce, and shared data standards and widely recognized benchmarks are still lacking.
3. **Exploration of domain-specific small models for GIS.** Lightweight, task-oriented models can complement general-purpose LLMs for well-defined GIS tasks at lower deployment cost.
4. **Integration with world models and real-world environments.** Coupling LLMs with physical process models, simulation, and real geospatial environments is needed for reliable reasoning about geographic processes.
5. **Improving interpretability and trustworthiness.** Hallucination and black-box behavior remain major barriers in high-stakes applications such as urban planning and disaster response.

Looking further ahead, the rigorous spatiotemporal logic and structured knowledge of GIS can in turn strengthen LLMs, pointing toward a bidirectional synergy between **LLM4GIS** and **GIS4LLM**.

## 🗂️ Full Paper List (All 673 Papers)

This list contains all 673 core research papers included in the survey (literature retrieved up to July 31, 2026), grouped by year and sorted alphabetically. Each study is counted once: papers first released on arXiv and later published are listed under their formal venue.

**Keywords Legend:** 🟦 **Method**: `Arch` (model architecture), `Token` (tokenization), `PT` (pretraining), `SFT` (supervised fine-tuning), `RL` (reinforcement learning), `Prompt` (prompt engineering), `RAG` (retrieval-augmented generation), `Agent` (LLM-based agents); 🟧 `Benchmark`: evaluation-oriented study.
